2.   RECOGNITION  
C.   TSD Mission Moment—Katarina Tasich, Troy Union Elementary  
     Writing Assignment  
Tonight, as part of our Troy School District Mission Moment, we recognize 3rd 
grader, Katarina Tasich, for her efforts to make the world a better place and 
taking a stand against bullying. During the month of December , Troy Union 
students wrote opinion pieces on a variety of topic s.  The students were excit ed 
about the project and wanted their voices to be heard.  M any of them reached 
out to the principal.  One topic that peeked Mr. Cottone’s interest was Katarina’s 
essay – she took a risk and shared that our community should do more to stop 
bullying.  A few days later Katarina formally shared her writing piece with him 
and outlined the steps that could be done.  The ideas were great but one really 
stood out.  She suggested that the entire school wear black around a unified 
message of “Blacking out Bullying.”  The message caught on at Tro y Union and 
throughout the entire school district as many community members wore black to 
support her message.  Katrina is proof that we can all make a difference. 
At this time, we would like to invite  Mr. Michael Cottone  and teacher Jess 
Bronsteen to come tell us a little more about Katarina’s efforts.   
Recommended Action:  Invite Kata up  to receive a certificate and 
congratulations from the Board. 
7
     2. D.   02.28.17 
2.   RECOGNITION  
D.   National Blue Ribbon Award – Schroeder Elementary School 
We are pleased to recognize Schroeder Elementary School this evening for their 
designation as a National Blue Ribbon School . This award honors Schroeder as 
one of the very best schools in the United States.  
U.S. Secretary of Education John B. King, Jr. recognized Schroeder in a ceremony in 
Washington, D. C. this past November . Just 3 29 schools from around the country 
were chosen for this honor in 2016. This award affirms the hard work of students, 
staff, parents, and t he Schroeder community.  As Secretary King stated, "National 
Blue Ribbon Schools are proof that we can prepare every child for college and 
meaningful careers.  Your schools are on the cutting edge, pioneering innovative 
educational practices —professional learning communities, project -based learning, 
social and emotional learning, positive behavior systems —making you shining 
examples for your communities, your state and the nation." 
Congratulations for this impressive designation! 
________________________________________________ 
Recommended Action:  Invite Brian Canfield (and Schroeder staff in 
attendance) to come up and receive congratulations from the Board and 
a certificate.

2.       RECOGNITION 
E.  Principal Appreciation Month 
Throughout the State of Michigan, February has traditionally been celebrated as 
Principal Appreciation Month, in recognition of the outstanding work of our 
building-level educational leaders. In Troy, we are fortunate to have an 
exemplary group of principals dedicated to the success of every student in the 
District. This evening, we will pass a resolution to commend our elementary and 
secondary principals for their ex ceptional commitment to excellence in the Troy 
School District.  
WHEREAS, energetic and inspiring school leadership is essential if teachers, 
schools and districts are to ensure that all Troy students are college  and career 
ready; and, 
WHEREAS, K-12 school principals play a vital role in the success of students 
and act as the liaison between the school and the community it serves, ensuring 
that parents and the community are aware of student and school achievements; 
and, 
WHEREAS, Troy School District pr incipals are dedicated,  quality educational 
professionals committed to providing sound leadership and guidance to their 
staff and students; and,  
WHEREAS, during this month, we join with educators, parents and students 
throughout Michigan to raise awaren ess of the importance of educational 
leadership, and to recognize and thank all principals working in Troy for their 
exemplary service; 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED , that the Troy Board of Education 
officially recognizes February as Principal Appreciation Month in Troy and thanks 
our dedicated building leaders for all they do for our students and staff.    
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution.

  Supported by:

WHEREAS, there may be in and may, hereafter from time 
to time, come into the hands of Rick West, Treasurer of 
Troy School District, certain public monies belonging to 
or held for the State, County, or other political units of 
the State or otherwise held according to law, and 
WHEREAS, under the laws of Michigan, this Board is 
required to provide by Resolution for the deposit of all 
public monies, including tax monies coming into the 
hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter 
called bank(s) to be designated in such Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that said Treasurer, 
Rick West, is hereby directed to deposit and/or invest all 
public monies, including tax monies now in or coming 
into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into 
the following depository institutions: 
Bank of America 
Bank of New York 
Charter One Bank 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Comerica Bank

By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third 
Bank)

and

institutions, provided it is in the interest of the District; 
and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that funds of the District may 
be invested according to Board Policy 5000.06; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer is 
authorized to combine money from more than one fund 
for the purpose of making an investment authorized by 
subsection (1) (h) of Section 380.1223 of the Revised 
School Code; and 
THE MAXIMUM AMOUNT TO BE INVESTED  with the 
above institutions is regulated by the school code.

was adopted by said Board at a meeting thereof held on 
the date specified above.

RECOGNITION  
celebrates the work of a very important group of 
individuals: The Board of Education. Troy is truly 
fortunate to be governed by Board members who 
dedicate an incredible amount of time and energy 
educational opportunities for all students. You are 
tireless, professional, and passionate and this community 
owes you a debt of gratitude for all that you do and all 
that you give. Tonight, Troy School District administration 
recognized our Board of Education members with a gift 
that represents our students and staff - the very people 
they work so hard to serve.  One of the first initiatives 
developed when Dr. Machesky was selected to lead this 
district was to develop the idea of ONE TROY—that we 
are not merely a district of schools, but ONE school 
district, with a single mission and a single focus on 
student achievement for ALL.  This evening, we are happy 
to present you each with a ONE Troy ensemble, a basket 
filled with a token of appreciation and pride in each of 
our schools. You will find t-shirts and hats, water bottles 
and mugs, pens and pencils—there are even some baked 
goods in there, each serving as a symbol of one of our 
outstanding schools. We hope that this will serve as a 
reminder of the impact you have on the lives of our 
students and staff.  From our more senior members to 
our newest trustees, we are grateful to have each and 
every one of you.  Thank you for all you do.

PERSONNEL 
Employment Dispute Resolution 
Board of Education of the Troy School District has 
determined that it is in the best interest of the District to 
establish an Employment Dispute Resolution Plan 
applicable to all applicants for employment, all substitute 
employees, all nonunion employees and all nonunion 
administrators and supervisors employed by the School 
District; and 
WHEREAS , the Revised School Code, MCL 380.11a 
permits a school district to establish an Employment 
Dispute Resolution Plan. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT : 
 1. The Board hereby authorizes the 
establishment of an Employment Dispute Resolution Plan 
applicable to all applicants for employment, all substitute 
employees, all nonunion employees and all nonunion 
administrators and supervisors employed by the School 
District.

BUSINESS 
District”) issued a Request for Proposals for Purchase of 
Real Property (“RFP”) to obtain proposals to purchase

 7. B.   02.28.17 
7. CURRICULUM 
B.  CONSIDERATION:   Schools Closed to Open Enrollment for  
                                        2017-2018 
Administration has reviewed the enrollment for elementary, middle, and high 
school attendance areas.  Due to the elementary and middle school  buildings’ 
total student enrollment and physical space constraints, we recommend that 
Bemis, Schroeder and Boulan Park Schools be closed to open enrollment.  Our 
goal for Athens and Troy High has been to maximize each building’s enrollment 
which has resulted in both schools being closed to open enrollment for the past 
several years. Based on current enrollment projections for the 2017-2018 school 
year, it is recommended that Athens and Troy High be closed to open enrollment 
with the exception of not more than (25) twenty -five incoming ninth grade 
students who reside in the Troy High School attendance area, but wish to attend 
Athens High School.  Applications for the (25) twenty-five spots at Athens High 
School will be accepted during a 2-week window at the end of March and early 
April. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Troy Board of Education adopts a resolution to close 
Bemis, Schroeder and Boulan Park Schools as well as Athens and Troy High Schools to 
open enrollment for the 2017-2018 school year, with the exception of not more than 
(25) twenty-five incoming ninth grade students who reside in the Troy High School 
attendance area , but wish to attend Athens High School.  Students with siblings 
attending Athens and Troy High and currently enrolled in grades 9 – 11 will be allowed 
to open enroll to remain for the 2017-2018 school year.  
Yes: __________ 
No: __________

Non-Schools of Choice Student (Current Grade/Year)
Projected Schools of Choice
Sibling Preference Placement for
Kindergarten, 1st Grade and Baker MS Students 
A survey was emailed to current Schools of Choice families requesting information on siblings
 who would be eligible for Kindergarten, 1st Grade and 6th, 7th and 8th Grade students for the 
2017-2018 School Year.
Number of Kindergarten students that plan to attend: 51
Number of 1st Grade students that plan to attend: 4
15 – 6th Grade
4 – 8th Grade
8
123
Number of  Middle School students that plan to attend:
7 – 7th Grade
The following factors were considered in making the recommendations for the Schools of Choice Program for 
2016-2017 school year:
 Current enrollment
 Enrollment projections, patterns and trends
 Numbers and percentages of Schools of Choice students currently in the district
 Projected number of Schools of Choice siblings who would be age-eligible to enter the district in 2016-2017 if the 
decision is made to admit a “Limited” number of kindergarten, first and sixth through eighth grade  students (Baker 
Middle School) under Schools of Choice
 Available elementary classrooms
After analyzing the data and factors referenced above, we recommend that the district:
 Use Fred Ignatovich’s 1.5 enrollment projection numbers, with his recommended formula (most likely + low/2) as 
the foundation for determining the number of Schools of Choice (SOC) students to admit for the 2016-2017 school 
year.  Please note when reviewing “Document 1,” that our “actual” enrollment as of the February Count for both 
kindergarten and first grade students was above both Mr. Ignatovich’s “Most Likely” and “Low” enrollment 2015-
16 projections.
 Accept SOC applications for 100 Kindergarten and 30 First Grade students.   Based upon the number of 
applications to kindergarten received for the 2015-2016 school year, we anticipate attracting more than 100 
applicants.  This would allow us to admit 100 kindergartners initially, with the option of admitting additional 
students, if space allows, in the third week of August.  While this is quite close to the opening of school, we have 
found in the past that some families still welcome the opportunity to enroll their child in the Troy School District.   
 Limit Schools of Choice kindergarten applicants to the full-day kindergarten program.  
 Accept “Sibling Preference” applications only for siblings of current SOC students (to attend Baker Middle School 
only).  Our current estimates for enrollment next year approximate to 728.  Accepting 15 applications during the 
initial application process will bring us extremely close to an ideal total student population at Baker, while allowing 
an option to admit more students in August if space permits.
DATE: February 28, 2017
TO: Dr. Richard Machesky, Superintendent
FROM: Kris Griffor
                      Mark Dziatczak
SUBJECT:   Schools of Choice Recommendations 
                          for the 2017-2018 School Year
The following factors were considered in making the recommendations for the Schools of Choice Program for 2017-2018 
school year:
• Current enrollment across the Troy School District
• Enrollment projections, patterns and trends
• Numbers and percentages of Schools of Choice students currently in the district
• Projected number of Schools of Choice siblings who would be age-eligible to enter the district in 2017-2018 if the 
decision is made to admit a “Limited” number of kindergarten, first and sixth through eighth grade students under Schools 
of Choice
• Available elementary classrooms
After analyzing the data and factors referenced above, we recommend that the district:
• Use Fred Ignatovich’s 1.5 enrollment projection numbers, with his recommended formula (most likely + low/2) as the 
foundation for determining the number of Schools of Choice (SOC) students to admit for the 2017-2018school year. 
• Accept SOC applications for 55 Kindergarten and 5 First Grade students.   Based upon the number of applications to 
kindergarten received for the 2016-2017 school year, we anticipate attracting many more than 50 applicants.  This would 
allow the District to admit 55 kindergartners initially, with the option of admitting additional students, if space allows, in 
the third week of August.  While this is quite close to the opening of school, we have found in the past that some families 
still welcome the opportunity to enroll their child in the Troy School District.   
• Accept SOC applications for 27 (total) 6th-8th Grade students. This will accommodate “Sibling Preference” students for 
current attending SOC students. Unlike in past years, SOC middle school students will be placed in the middle school that 
corresponds with their current sibling learning path.

D. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice Section 105 - Elementary  
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts shall pass a 
resolution each school year if they intend  to implement a Schools of 
Choice program for non -resident students who attend another school 
district within Oakland County.  I t would be appropriate , for the 2017-
2018 school year , for the Board of Education to consider adopting a 
resolution for Schools of Choice Section 105 for (55) fifty-five kindergarten 
and (5) five first grade nonresident students who attend a school district 
within Oakland County  (total students includ e non Oakland County 
residents – section 105c). 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School District, 
exercising the option permitted by Section 105 of the State School Aid Act of 1979, 
as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a limited Schools 
of Choice program for the 2017-2018 school year; for (55) fifty-five nonresident 
kindergarten and (5) five nonresident first grade students who attend a school 
district within Oakland County (total students includ e non Oakland County 
residents – section 105c).

E. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice Section 105c - Elementary 
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts shall pass a 
resolution each school year if they intend to implement a Schools of Choice 
program for non -resident students who attend another school district 
whose county is contiguous with Oakland County.  It would be appropriate, 
for the 2017-2018 school year , for the Board of Education to consider 
adopting a resolution for Schools of Choice Section 105c for (55) fifty-five 
kindergarten and (5) five first grade nonresident students who attend a 
school district  that is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School 
District boundar ies (total student s include Oakland County residents  – 
section 105). 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION

 Supported by: __________________________

exercising the option permitted by Section 105c of the State School Aid Act of 1979, 
as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a limited Schools 
of Choice program for the 2017-2018 school year; for (55) fifty- five nonresident 
kindergarten and (5) five  nonresident first grade students who attend a school 
district that is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School District boundaries 
(total students include Oakland County residents – section 105).

F. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice Grades 6-8 - Section 105 
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts shall pass a resolution each 
school year if they intend to participate in a Schools of Choice program.  It is 
anticipated that seats will be available for the 2017-2018 school year for grades 
6th - 8th at Baker, Larson and Smith  Middle Schools.  It would be appropriate at 
this time to consider adopting a Schools of Choice section 105 resolution for (20) 
twenty nonresident 6th - 8th grade students at Baker, (6) six nonresident 6th - 8th 
grade students at Larson and (1) one nonresident 6th - 8th grade student at Smith 
Middle School who attend a district within Oakland County (total students includes 
non-Oakland county residents – section 105c).

8. A. 02.28.17 
8. BUSINESS
A. CONSIDERATION:  Bus Purchase
Ten of the district’s school buses  have reached end-o f-life.  Th e 
Michigan School Business Officials (MSBO) School Bus Purchasing  
Program was used to obtain competitive pricing.  Pricing was 
compared and reviewed by Mr. Dominick Asaro, Transportation 
Liaison.  It is recommended the bid be awarded as stated below. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Be it therefore resolved that the bid for school buses be awarded to Hoekstra 
Transportation in the amount of $885,322.00 and that this amount be approved 
as an expenditure of the 2016 Capital Projects Fund.

     DATE:     January 5, 2017 
Phone 248 823-4091 
Fax  248 823-4013 
School Bus Replacement Program - 2014 Bond Series 2 - 2016-17 School Year  
Ten of the District’s bus fleet have reached their end-of-life cycle. Michigan School Business 
Officials, MSBO, offers a cooperative bus purchasing service to obtain competitive pricing 
from bidding dealers.  
This service was utilized to obtain school bus pricing for several different  models that match 
our District’s bus needs. After analyzing the information provided from three proposals and 
reviewing these proposals  with Dominick Asaro, Transportation Liaison, collaboratively we 
recommend the low bidder, Hoekstra Transportation.   
It is therefore recommended that Hoekstra Transportation, be awarded the school bus purchase   
in the amount of $885,322.00, as detailed in the attached bid tabulation.
